Establish equivalent semantic/finite guarantees at the intended public boundaries and test malformed model/snapshot entry paths. Records: [017](factor/credit-snapshots.md), [019](factor/credit-artifacts.md).

- **Observed — Forecast descriptors and risk-measure validation need consistent boundaries.** factor_model_at installs a supplied risk measure without validating it. VolHorizon parsing directly casts u64 n_steps to usize, yielding platform-dependent behavior for values above u32 on 32-bit targets; extra descriptor keys are accepted and years takes precedence. Reconcile domain/precedence/portability with accepted boundary policy. Records: [018](factor/credit-horizons.md).

- **Investigate — Forecast config can mix systematic and residual horizons.** factor_model_at scales config.covariance but clones matching issuer rows unchanged; portfolio FactorModel squares their adder_vol_annualized and adds residual variance without a horizon multiplier. Years(h!=1) can therefore combine h-scaled systematic covariance with annual residual variance, even though the separate idiosyncratic_vol helper scales correctly. Confirm the static control-flow difference with an end-to-end reproduction and agree one horizon contract. Records: [018](factor/credit-horizons.md); portfolio consumer: [013](portfolio-attribution/13-credit-residual-risk-and-reporting.md).

- **Decision — Historical ES and tail-stress allocations differ.** Historical ES shares fractional probability mass equally across boundary ties; stress selects the first whole ceil-tail scenarios with equal weight, so boundary-tie stress can depend on input order and differ from ES. Stress permits one observation and returns empty results before confidence validation, while historical risk requires two and validates confidence first. Give these distinct calculations explicit labels/domain rules. Specify gain-tail behavior and qualify stress summary ratios before use as risk controls. Records: [008](factor/position-historical-risk.md), [009](factor/tail-stress.md).

- **Decision — Credit calibration clock and default conventions.** Returns means basis-point differences, not proportional returns; annualization uses 252/12/4. Daily spacing accepts weekday gaps up to seven calendar days without a holiday calendar or elapsed-time weighting. Default Monthly/Returns/DTS/GloballyOff/Sample/FullSampleRepaired settings require an explicit supported-use convention and evidence across frequencies. Specify accepted spread range and minimum sample size, and qualify fixed-duration weighting on the intended source data. Records: [011](factor/credit-panel.md).

- **Decision — DTS weights and as-of anchors.** Returns-mode DTS weights use beginning-of-interval spreads; Levels uses current-date spreads, with one fixed duration per issuer. As-of must be the final history date but supplied as-of spreads may differ from final history observations. Agree contemporaneous/lagged weighting, duration treatment and anchor reconciliation. Records: [011](factor/credit-panel.md), [017](factor/credit-snapshots.md).

- **Decision — Hierarchy fold policy.** Bucket counts include all members, the default missing-threshold value is five, and hierarchy levels fold independently without parent refitting. Preserve executable folds and specify eligibility/counting, threshold calibration and what a folded factor represents before adopting the hierarchy. Specify effective sample requirements and quantify membership-threshold discontinuities; preserve operative fold records across artifact consumers. Records: [012](factor/credit-buckets.md).

- **Decision — Sequential OLS peel and fallback conventions.** Centered OLS slopes are removed without an intercept; issuer level betas are fitted against leave-one-out means but applied to inclusive means. Degenerate, non-refining or insufficient fits fall back to one; folded betas stay zero and optional shrinkage targets one. Raw-residual R² may be negative while histograms clamp to [0,1]. Agree interpretation, fallback diagnostics and independent statistical qualification. Records: [013](factor/credit-beta-peel.md).

- **Decision — Residual variance estimator semantics.** Sample uses demeaned n−1 variance; EWMA uses normalized finite-window zero-mean squares, and Levels mode estimates volatility from changes. Fewer than two observations returns zero. Specify the intended estimator/domain and distinguish insufficient evidence from an economically measured zero, with frequency and regime validation. Records: [014](factor/credit-variance.md).

- **Decision — Residual-volatility source cascade.** Selection is override, own history, deepest/parent arithmetic mean peer volatility, global mean, then zero. Near-zero residual amplitude excludes history, while a constant nonzero residual can qualify yet have zero sample variance; the default conflates global and no-history zero source cases. Agree source priority, pooling and diagnostics without treating fallback as empirical calibration. Specify minimum peer population and quantify out-of-sample error and concentration from substitutions. Records: [015](factor/credit-residual-volatility.md).

- **Decision — Covariance strategy and diagnostic authority.** Repaired pairwise correlation uses selected Sample/EWMA marginal variances; Ridge adds annualized bp² alpha; LedoitWolf supplies the entire systematic sample-shrinkage covariance independently of the selected volatility model. Diagnostic vol_state can differ from authoritative covariance, and Ridge’s static correlation remains pre-ridge by design. Preserve stored-covariance authority while disclosing estimator/repair provenance and qualifying repair magnitude. Specify overlap, regularization and repair thresholds, repair-distance limits, conditioning and held-out covariance evidence. Records: [016](factor/credit-covariance.md), [018](factor/credit-horizons.md), [019](factor/credit-artifacts.md).

- **Decision — New-issuer snapshot weighting and period population.** Unknown names use unit betas/runtime tags. With DTS they lack new-name duration and are excluded from weighted means when known weighted names exist; an all-unweighted bucket falls back to equal weighting. Period decomposition includes only intersection keys, omitting entries/exits. Agree treatment of new issuers and changing populations; shared-kernel anchor parity only proves internal consistency. Quantify sensitivity to membership and model changes while preserving operative fold metadata. Records: [017](factor/credit-snapshots.md).

- **Decision — Forecast vocabulary denotes annualized periods.** OneStep and Unconditional mean one annualized period; NSteps(n) multiplies annualized variance by n rather than dividing by panel frequency. This is documented behavior, not a new code/doc mismatch. Require fractional years or explicit periods_per_year for shorter horizons, and qualify flat EWMA forecasting that incorporates no new observations. Qualify square-root-time assumptions against intended horizons and serial dependence. Records: [018](factor/credit-horizons.md).

- **Decision — Risk coordinate and residual-overlay contract.** Covariance coordinates/horizon and exposure/reporting currency remain caller responsibilities. The residual overlay accepts signed individual variance allocations provided their aggregate is nonnegative, and adds systematic and residual variance. Agree unit/alignment guarantees, additivity/dependence assumptions and allocation interpretation before consuming combined contributions. Set scale-aware error tolerances and independently verify conservation and finite-difference marginals for hedged, near-zero and high-dynamic-range portfolios. Reconcile forged/inconsistent decomposition validation and the zero-overlay branch. Records: [001](factor/factor-configuration.md), [005](factor/factor-risk.md), [006](factor/residual-risk.md).

- **Decision — Budget policy and normalization.** Budget targets need only sum within 0.05 and are not renormalized; the default breach threshold is 1.20. These are control policies rather than empirically qualified limits. Specify accepted tolerance, signed contribution handling and breach/reporting semantics, alongside the finite-sum investigation. Include zero-target behavior, report conservation and calculation provenance in the control contract. Records: [010](factor/risk-budgets-and-reports.md).

- **Evidence — Credit factor methods need independent statistical provenance.** Hand Sample/EWMA/LedoitWolf fixtures, leave-one-out beta examples, DTS examples and fractional/tied historical ES cases provide specific formula evidence. They do not establish out-of-sample behavior or regime robustness. Attach primary provenance for the bespoke hierarchical peel, fallback/statistical choices and calibration studies; broad Meucci/Tasche/Hallerbach/RiskMetrics/LedoitWolf/BenDor citations do not certify the whole pipeline. Record methodology/config provenance for shrinkage, thresholds, covariance strategy and numerical controls. Include hierarchy-order and degeneracy choices, held-out beta/residual behavior across bucket sizes, near-one EWMA lambda and serial dependence, and peer-substitution error/concentration. Records: [008](factor/position-historical-risk.md), [011](factor/credit-panel.md), [012](factor/credit-buckets.md), [013](factor/credit-beta-peel.md), [014](factor/credit-variance.md), [015](factor/credit-residual-volatility.md), [016](factor/credit-covariance.md), [017](factor/credit-snapshots.md).

- **Decision — Matching rule and unknown-issuer governance.** Adopt ordered-rule governance and hierarchy-depth limits for external configs, then qualify portfolio rulebooks against actual dependency inventories. Specify allowed unknown-issuer behavior and declared-factor handling at each consumer; keep delimiter/custom-dimension identity consistent across calibration, matching and snapshots, and quantify generic-only/unit-beta fallback error. Matching coverage alone does not establish sensitivity coverage. Records: [003](factor/dependency-matching.md), [004](factor/credit-matching.md).

- **Decision — Downstream bump-domain and unit ownership.** Trace the authoritative bump-domain validation in each sensitivity engine, qualify absolute vol-point versus relative-percent shifts and custom-factor units, and reconcile uncovered public inputs under the accepted validation-boundary policy. Records: [001](factor/factor-configuration.md).

## Covenants

[Scope inventory](covenants/README.md).

- **Observed — Project-finance lockup ordering differs from its documented contract.** `ProjectFinance.distribution_lockup_dscr` is described as higher than default DSCR, but `project_finance` checks only individual finiteness/nonnegativity; `templates_accept_valid_thresholds` explicitly accepts lockup 1.1 below default 1.2. Resolve the intended ordering and align validation, examples and contract-specific template qualification. The source/test difference is confirmed; no template was executed. Decide cross-threshold constraints beyond the stated lockup/default relationship, including total versus senior leverage, without assuming every ordering must be prohibited. Record: [COVENANTS-11](covenants/11-package-templates.md).

- **Observed — Dated-series calendar lookup does not enforce its trait's unknown-period contract.** `DatedMetricSeries::period_end_date` reconstructs a date from `period.year()` and `period.index()` without checking daily frequency or membership in the stored rows, although `ModelTimeSeries` says unknown periods fail. Convenience wrappers use owned periods, but generic calls can supply an unrelated period; inactive/empty test sets may never force a missing-value lookup. Reconcile the date resolver and qualify generic inactive/unknown-period cases. Also decide empty-evaluation validation and caller ownership of observation order and forecast eligibility; include unknown-frequency periods with inactive or empty engines. Record: [COVENANTS-07](covenants/07-dated-series.md).

- **Observed — Effective terms and report provenance are only partially represented.** Report/forecast descriptions come from the static covenant type while numeric thresholds can be amended or scheduled. Report metadata is created from default `FinstackConfig`, not upstream metric configuration; no rounding context is applied to the comparisons, and forecast output omits resolved volatility/seed/reference provenance. Align descriptive versus effective terms and define which metadata actually attests to computation. `validate` also claims ordered windows while code only checks pairwise nonoverlap; reconcile that smaller contract difference. Records: [COVENANTS-02](covenants/02-effective-terms.md), [COVENANTS-12](covenants/12-reports-and-json.md).

- **Investigate — Breach history has no chronological replay protection.** Tracking validates only before mutation, selects uncured episodes at/before the requested date and records no cure date or last-evaluation date. Static control flow allows a same-day fail/cure/fail sequence to append the same identity/date after the first episode was cured; later validation would then reject duplicate episode keys. Backdated calls can also observe later boolean cure state. Reproduce those transitions and define same-day idempotency, chronology and multiple-episode import rules before choosing a fix. Record: [COVENANTS-05](covenants/05-breach-and-cure.md).

- **Investigate — Finite accepted stochastic inputs do not bound intermediate arithmetic.** Analytic risk forms `threshold/base`, `sigma*sigma*T` and `1-Phi(z)` without stable log-ratio/survival branches or a finite-output post-check. Monte Carlo exponentiates unchecked drift/shock expressions; NaN samples are counted as breaches by the shared comparator. Large finite sigma/scale ratios and rare upper tails therefore need independent reproduction and domain/tolerance decisions. Qualify headroom overflow and the `abs(threshold)<EPSILON` absolute-distance switch too. No numerical failure was executed in this audit. Cover signed zero, zero/tiny thresholds and contradictory positive ratios with negative denominators. Compare analytic and Monte Carlo extreme-intermediate behavior rather than inferring tail agreement from the moderate fixture. Records: [COVENANTS-03](covenants/03-comparison-and-headroom.md), [COVENANTS-09](covenants/09-analytic-probability.md), [COVENANTS-10](covenants/10-monte-carlo.md).

- **Decision — A passing report can mean no contractual assessment occurred.** Inactive, fully waived and unmet-springing tests return passes without numeric values; active `Negative`/`Affirmative` types also pass without evaluating the restriction and without an unsupported-assessment detail. Batch forecasts omit those types. Decide explicit assessment status and qualitative-check ownership, especially for the CovLite secured-debt restriction. Test frequency is metadata and incurrence action completion is caller-supplied context, not engine-verified evidence. Decide cadence ownership, evidence of executed incurrence actions, and whether uncertain springing triggers belong within forecast scope. Decide whether absent headroom distinguishes inactive, indeterminate and overflow cases, with [COVENANTS-03](covenants/03-comparison-and-headroom.md) supplying that boundary. Records: [COVENANTS-04](covenants/04-activation-and-scope.md), [COVENANTS-11](covenants/11-package-templates.md).

- **Decision — Calculation-ready metrics need an adopted accounting and monetary boundary.** Ratios and amounts are untyped f64 inputs; the engine neither constructs LTM values nor reconciles currency, earnings adjustments or ratio/denominator consistency. Net leverage requires an earnings denominator, but gross leverage can omit it and minimum coverage cannot carry this denominator field. Comparisons are exact without contractual rounding; map duplicates replace and custom/basket fallback names can be empty. Specify accepted provenance, negative/indeterminate policies and amount precision consistently with accepted units and monetary-arithmetic decisions. Choose duplicate-input-key treatment and reconcile empty fallback names with the explicit-name validation rule; retain contract-specific metric/denominator/currency/LTM provenance requirements. Records: [COVENANTS-01](covenants/01-terms-and-metric-selection.md), [COVENANTS-03](covenants/03-comparison-and-headroom.md).

- **Decision — Effective-term and cure choices require contract-specific adoption.** Windows fully replace base specs; outside windows base specs resume; waiver/schedule intervals are inclusive and negative finite amendments are accepted. Full waivers and inactive tests do not cure an episode or suppress its later captured consequences. `None` cure period means immediate consequence eligibility and no automatic numerical cure; zero days protects the breach date, and passing after an expired deadline does not cure. Decide those meanings, orphan waiver handling and identity continuity across replacement sets. Qualify None versus zero-day grace explicitly and define the evidence required for cure and imported episodes, including the templates' inherited cure periods. Records: [COVENANTS-02](covenants/02-effective-terms.md), [COVENANTS-05](covenants/05-breach-and-cure.md).

- **Decision — Consequence ordering and partial success need a durable consumer contract.** Each action commits separately on a target clone; a later error preserves earlier target/history commits but returns no applications vector, and history stores no successful-action dates. Cross-episode order is caller order, so setter sweeps/maturity changes can conflict; no maximum-sweep or precedence rule is applied. Qualify clone isolation and persistent audit reporting. The inspected TermLoan adapter rounds rate changes to whole bp and rejects realized default, nonzero percentage sweeps and collateral requests without richer typed inputs, so declared template actions do not prove executability. Qualify fractional-basis-point and monetary conversion under [ADR 0020](conventions/0020-numerical-representation-and-rounding.md), and resolve competing template sweep actions within the selected order contract. Record: [COVENANTS-06](covenants/06-consequence-execution.md).

- **Decision — Stochastic risk is a marginal overlay, with narrower scope than a breach/default model.** The method uses one supplied annual log-volatility, calendar days/365.25, positive-base GBM shocks and deterministic nonpositive values/activation/denominators. First breach and minimum headroom remain base-case quantities; batch output always includes base breaches and includes active zero-risk dates when cutoff is zero. MC restarts seed/streams per spec group, and adding eligible dates changes later draws; it supplies no cross-window/cross-covenant joint risk. Adopt the model, clock and presentation limits explicitly before using results as first-passage/default or portfolio probabilities. Resolve baseline-breach naming versus marginal risk, cutoff-zero behavior and uncertainty disclosure in batch output. Qualify single/batch calendar parity near the minimum supported date, where deterministic batch still calls `previous_day`. Records: [COVENANTS-08](covenants/08-forecast-composition.md), [COVENANTS-09](covenants/09-analytic-probability.md), [COVENANTS-10](covenants/10-monte-carlo.md).

- **Evidence — Probability qualification and package provenance remain specific gaps.** Inspected analytic/MC tests use a moderate near-threshold fixture, approximate analytic tolerance 0.001, internal MC agreement tolerance 0.005, and a single antithetic-error comparison. They do not independently qualify multi-date/group consistency, odd-path requests, rare-event zero-hit error, statistical coverage, extreme scales or fitted borrower-metric volatility. Named templates embed 30/60-day cures, 200bp increases and 100%/50% sweeps without identified agreement samples or empirical adoption evidence. Obtain independent numerical cases and contract/calibration provenance; this audit ran no simulations. Records: [COVENANTS-09](covenants/09-analytic-probability.md), [COVENANTS-10](covenants/10-monte-carlo.md), [COVENANTS-11](covenants/11-package-templates.md).

- **Evidence — Schema/serde success is not semantic restoration qualification.** Ten roots are registered, but inspected schema assertions specifically exercise an engine with tuple-shaped threshold schedules. Public fields and derived serde bypass most business constructors; ordinary forecast restoration does not check vector alignment/probability consistency. `HashMapMetricSource` and `ConsequenceApplication` lack the strict unknown-field declaration used by most other roots, and the registered wrapped metric source differs from the bare-map convenience parser. Qualify each intended host entry contract and result restoration boundary; do not equate structural report canonicalization with financial re-evaluation. Include cross-root unknown-field strictness and all generated consumer declaration surfaces alongside wrapped-versus-bare metric forms and semantic result consistency. Records: [COVENANTS-12](covenants/12-reports-and-json.md), [COVENANTS-13](covenants/13-schema-contracts.md).

## Features

[Scope inventory](features/README.md).

- **Observed — Timestamp chronology claim lacks its year-domain restriction.** `naive_datetime_order_key` formats `datetime.year()` with `{:04}` and neither formatter rejects negative years or an enabled expanded-year domain. The documentation says keys sort chronologically, but this lexical representation only supports that claim within an appropriate fixed-width nonnegative year domain; it does not encode signed years in chronological order. Restrict/document the supported year domain or adopt a sortable representation, and qualify UTC conversion at supported datetime boundaries. No date-boundary runtime was executed. Record: [FEATURES-001](features/01-ordering-keys-and-partitions.md); source: `finstack-quant/features/src/keys.rs`, both public formatter functions.

- **Observed — Panel module prose describes a different JSON result shape.** The module intro says `transform_panel_json` returns an object mapping operation names to columns; `PanelTransformResult`, serialization, checked-in schema and tests instead produce one ordered `columns` array of `{name, values}` entries. Reconcile the existing source documentation with the observed wire contract; this audit does not propose changing that contract or add a compatibility form. Record: [FEATURES-003](features/03-pipeline-and-wire-contract.md); source: `finstack-quant/features/src/panel.rs`, module intro and `transform_panel_json`; schema: `finstack-quant/features/schemas/features/1/panel_transform_result.schema.json`.

- **Investigate — Finite-output checks do not qualify accumulation error.** Mean/SD use scaled compensated helpers, but rolling sums and higher-moment reductions use ordinary addition. The inspected sum regression exercises a truly unrepresentable sum, not mixed-sign data with a representable mathematical result and overflowing/cancelling intermediates; unit sweeps do not test near-constant subnormal spread loss. Independently measure accuracy and avoidable overflow on these distinct routes before selecting a numerical error budget. No failing vector was executed. Include asymmetric and long-window higher moments, normalized near-constant z-scores, mixed-sign cancellation, and subnormal spread loss against high-precision expectations. Records: [FEATURES-004](features/04-scaled-statistics-and-quantiles.md), [FEATURES-006](features/06-rolling-aggregates.md), [FEATURES-008](features/08-rolling-shape-moments.md); source: `types.rs::{scaled_centered,mean,std}`, `timeseries.rs::rolling`, `timeseries/advanced.rs::{skewness,excess_kurtosis}`.

- **Investigate — Scale restoration and raw ratios need extreme-domain qualification.** Covariance restores `(cov*a)*b`, beta restores `((cov/var)*a)/b`, inverse-risk weights validate raw `signal/vol` before scale-neutral normalization, and EWMA score uses raw `(x-new_mean)/SD`. These evaluation orders can encounter an unrepresentable intermediate even when a final dimensionless/combined-scale result may be representable; underflow to finite zero is not caught. Reproduce with an independent high-precision reference and decide supported domains or a stable evaluation route. This is an investigation, not a reproduced defect. Include unequal-unit and near-degenerate covariance/beta pairs, tiny positive volatility, strongly unbalanced signal weights, and EWMA opposite-sign near-limit inputs and extreme spans. Agree per-partition net/gross tolerances for ordinary signal weights as well as their raw-ratio domain. Records: [FEATURES-013](features/13-ewma-mean-volatility-and-scores.md), [FEATURES-017](features/17-signal-to-weights.md), [FEATURES-019](features/19-pairwise-rolling-statistics.md); sources: `timeseries.rs::EwmaState::zscore`, `multi.rs::{risk_scaled_weights,pairwise_value}`.

- **Investigate — Intermediate nonfiniteness can be consumed before output validation.** `rolling_sharpe` tests only `std>0`, so an infinite internal sample SD takes the ratio branch and may produce a finite zero; Hampel compares against `threshold*scaled_mad` without checking that product, so an infinite cutoff suppresses replacement while retaining a finite current value. The public scan rejects only nonfinite emitted values, not these internal states. Qualify whether these limits are intentional domain behavior and add independently expected boundary cases; do not infer that every successful output certifies finite intermediates. Cover near-zero Sharpe dispersion and large SD/subtraction intermediates; qualify return/cash unit guidance. Include Hampel threshold equality, even-size Type-7 medians, subnormal spreads, and repeated filtering. Records: [FEATURES-010](features/10-rolling-period-sharpe.md), [FEATURES-022](features/22-hampel-filter.md); source: `finstack-quant/features/src/timeseries/advanced.rs::{rolling_sharpe,hampel_value}`. No numerical reproduction was run.

- **Investigate — Capped-allocation identities lack a final residual/tolerance check.** The kernel checks side capacity, uses descending magnitudes/suffix sums and updates remaining exposure with a zero floor, but checks only final finiteness after allocation. The hand four-row fixture verifies net/gross/cap identities; no inspected test qualifies large/skewed partitions, exact side-capacity boundaries or near-zero centered signs. Compare against an independent constrained-allocation oracle and agree final net/gross/cap tolerance and failure behavior. Agree centered-sign preservation, the constant-allocation zero exception, and side-infeasibility policy alongside the numerical tolerances. Record: [FEATURES-018](features/18-capped-neutral-weights.md); source: `cross_sectional.rs::cap_weights`. No breach of the target identities was reproduced.

- **Investigate — Integer domains exceed some downstream representations.** Positive controls admit any target-representable usize; exponential decay casts finite-observation age to i32 for `powi`, and bucket counts/labels are converted to f64. Thus the parser's domain alone does not ensure an exact exponent or integer label, and maximum accepted integers vary between native and wasm32. Select meaningful supported count bounds and qualify conversion/error behavior without allocating multi-billion-row examples. Records: [FEATURES-002](features/02-parameters-missing-and-failure.md), [FEATURES-012](features/12-exponential-current-row-weight.md), [FEATURES-015](features/15-cross-sectional-ranks-and-normal-scores.md); sources: `types.rs::usize_param`, `timeseries/advanced.rs::exponential_decay_weights`, `cross_sectional.rs::quantile_bucket`.

- **Decision — Agree the distinct clocks and duplicate-row semantics.** Lags/returns and EWMA advance on finite observations; rolling membership counts rows; decay has row membership but finite ages; trend uses row positions preserving gaps. Duplicate entity/order pairs remain separate observations ordered by input index, and duplicate entities within a cross-section receive separate equal representation. Decide whether these are the supported feature contracts and specify caller data-preparation requirements; no calendar-duration, duplicate rejection or aggregation is currently selected. Adopt the decay output as a current-row weight rather than a complete normalized column, and qualify trend at large response scales/long row positions and missing current rows against independent trends. Records: [FEATURES-001](features/01-ordering-keys-and-partitions.md), [FEATURES-005](features/05-finite-observation-returns-and-lags.md), [FEATURES-009](features/09-rolling-row-trend.md), [FEATURES-012](features/12-exponential-current-row-weight.md).

- **Decision — Agree operation-specific zero, missing and invalid-domain outcomes.** All nonfinite numeric inputs are excluded, but unestimable outputs differ: same-sign negative log levels are accepted; zero-prior/opposite-sign log domains yield None; nonpositive drawdown levels yield None while preserving peak; zero-risk Sharpe/correlation/beta and zero-MAD robust scores yield 0; zero-MAD Hampel replaces a nonzero deviation. These distinctions are observed choices, not adopted equivalents. Agree each financial/signal interpretation and preserve ADR0009's distinction without importing analytics-only contiguous-panel rules. Include whole-call failure behavior, default-window/sample floors, constant rolling z-scores and higher moments, singleton cross-sections, EWMA initialization and mature zero-volatility outcomes, and degenerate zero signal-weight allocations with their volatility-domain rules. Qualify explicit-null/unknown-key rejection and public error mapping on each host. Records additionally cover [FEATURES-006](features/06-rolling-aggregates.md), [FEATURES-008](features/08-rolling-shape-moments.md), [FEATURES-013](features/13-ewma-mean-volatility-and-scores.md), and [FEATURES-017](features/17-signal-to-weights.md). Records: [FEATURES-002](features/02-parameters-missing-and-failure.md), [FEATURES-005](features/05-finite-observation-returns-and-lags.md), [FEATURES-010](features/10-rolling-period-sharpe.md), [FEATURES-011](features/11-positive-level-drawdown.md), [FEATURES-014](features/14-cross-sectional-standardization.md), [FEATURES-019](features/19-pairwise-rolling-statistics.md), [FEATURES-022](features/22-hampel-filter.md).

- **Decision — Agree estimator and filter conventions.** Rolling scores use sample SD while cross-sectional scores/sigma clipping use population SD; rank uses lowest ties, percentile/normal scores use average tied open positions; buckets may be empty; quantiles use Type-7 interpolation. Inclusive windows include the observation being scored or filtered, and repeated statistical clipping can change results. Record the intended variants, use domains and non-idempotent cleaning expectations rather than treating library names as adoption evidence. Include the shared mean clamp and quantile convention, biased EWMA variance and initialization, and explicit Hampel threshold/zero-MAD controls. [FEATURES-004](features/04-scaled-statistics-and-quantiles.md), [FEATURES-013](features/13-ewma-mean-volatility-and-scores.md), and [FEATURES-022](features/22-hampel-filter.md) supply these additional choices. Records: [FEATURES-007](features/07-rolling-order-statistics.md), [FEATURES-014](features/14-cross-sectional-standardization.md), [FEATURES-015](features/15-cross-sectional-ranks-and-normal-scores.md), [FEATURES-016](features/16-cross-sectional-clipping.md).

- **Decision — Agree OLS rank, exact-fit suppression and failure policies.** The SVD requires full rank at `EPSILON*max(n,p)*smax`, allows n=p, and suppresses residuals within `8*EPSILON*max(n,p)` times evaluation magnitude. Cross-sectional no-fit fails the whole call; rolling no-fit emits None, while SVD numerical errors still fail. Rolling residuals include the current row in its own fit, and neutralize-and-zscore fixes the intercept on. Decide these policies, including no-exposure variants and whether callers need reason/conditioning diagnostics, without silently substituting regularization or forecast residuals. Records: [FEATURES-020](features/20-scaled-svd-neutralization.md), [FEATURES-021](features/21-rolling-regression-residuals.md); source: `multi.rs::{fit_ols,residual_for_idx,neutralize,rolling_regression_residual,neutralize_and_zscore}`.

- **Decision — Decide the typed/serde trust boundary and result provenance contract.** Public spec/result fields and derived Deserialize enforce shape rather than pipeline invariants. Empty pipelines return no columns without checking unused keys; nonempty pipelines only check required columns on use; forged results can have duplicate names or unequal lengths, and `get_column` returns the first match without validation. Results omit original keys and operation metadata. Determine whether execution-only validation with caller-owned alignment/provenance is intended, then qualify those boundaries. Exercise empty pipelines, unused malformed key columns, whitespace-distinct names, forged result lookup, and preserved caller alignment/provenance at the retained boundary. Record: [FEATURES-003](features/03-pipeline-and-wire-contract.md); source: `panel.rs::{transform_panel,PanelTransformSpec,PanelTransformResult::get_column}`. This is not a claim that successful transform outputs violate their validated contract.

- **Evidence — Numerical evidence is narrower than estimator breadth.** Inspected scale sweeps mostly compare the same production kernel at different units; pinned moments, robust scores and normal quartiles are small authored fixtures without a documented independent computation/version for every value. The feature tests have no independent numeric rolling-covariance fixture; drawdown and decay are represented by simple ordinary cases rather than missing/nonpositive/limiting-domain qualification. Add independently sourced expectations and justified per-method tolerances for the specified variants, preserving the role of current invariant tests. Include signed-level log fallback at extreme ratios and simple-return cancellation near one ([FEATURES-005](features/05-finite-observation-returns-and-lags.md)); missing-current/singleton/equal-bound/sparse rolling ranks and filters; all-missing or nonpositive drawdown paths, recovery after zero, duplicate timestamps and extreme positive ratios; near-zero/near-one decay, sparse windows and denominator accuracy; extreme demeaning; inverse-normal expectations for large partitions; and large finite sigma/level clipping, equal bounds, all-missing data and repeated filtering ([FEATURES-016](features/16-cross-sectional-clipping.md)). Records: [FEATURES-004](features/04-scaled-statistics-and-quantiles.md), [FEATURES-007](features/07-rolling-order-statistics.md), [FEATURES-008](features/08-rolling-shape-moments.md), [FEATURES-011](features/11-positive-level-drawdown.md), [FEATURES-012](features/12-exponential-current-row-weight.md), [FEATURES-014](features/14-cross-sectional-standardization.md), [FEATURES-015](features/15-cross-sectional-ranks-and-normal-scores.md), [FEATURES-019](features/19-pairwise-rolling-statistics.md), [FEATURES-022](features/22-hampel-filter.md); evidence: `finstack-quant/features/tests/{transforms,audit_regressions}.rs`, inline `types.rs` constant tests.

- **Evidence — SVD cutoff and residual suppression lack independent threshold qualification.** Current hand fits, scaled-exposure invariance, duplicate-column rejection and exact-line zeros are useful but do not characterize near-collinear designs, multiple exposure scales or tiny economically real residuals around the suppression threshold. Produce independent fit/reference evidence with matrix conditioning, rank expectations, threshold sweeps and explicit tolerances; check standardized neutrality after suppression rather than assuming exact orthogonality survives. For rolling fits, include missing current rows, intercept-only/no-exposure variants, exactly determined windows, and rank transitions against independent fits. Records: [FEATURES-020](features/20-scaled-svd-neutralization.md), [FEATURES-021](features/21-rolling-regression-residuals.md); evidence: `audit_regressions.rs::{ols_residuals_are_invariant_to_exposure_units,standardized_residuals_preserve_neutrality_and_do_not_amplify_roundoff,rolling_regression_requires_the_configured_complete_row_count}`.

- **Evidence — One pinned EWMA vector is not broad cross-target qualification.** `libm::hypot` reduces platform-specific behavior, and the regression pins five volatility values to bits documented as captured from WASM. Reading the fixture does not establish a current two-target run, build provenance or identity across spans, extreme observations and missing histories; other transforms also use exp/log/normal quantiles/SVD. Qualify the claimed supported conditions with native/WASM build/runtime provenance and justified tolerances under the accepted reproducibility tiers. Record: [FEATURES-013](features/13-ewma-mean-volatility-and-scores.md); evidence: `audit_regressions.rs::ewma_vol_is_bit_identical_across_build_targets`, [INVARIANTS.md](../../../INVARIANTS.md) section 2.1. No cross-target execution was performed.

## Arrow interchange

[Scope inventory](arrow-interchange/README.md).

- **Observed — Direct Arrow and envelope JSON have different nonfinite semantics.** Direct export retains `Some(NaN/±∞)` as present floating values. Core envelope variants derive ordinary serde, whose pinned JSON serializer emits nonfinite floats as null: a nullable round trip loses present-versus-missing identity, while a nonnullable float vector cannot restore that null as f64. This is static serializer/type evidence, not an executed round trip. Reconcile both transport contracts with ADR 0009 and qualify nullable/nonnullable paths. Records: [001](arrow-interchange/01-typed-values-and-null-semantics.md).

- **Observed — Raw envelopes bypass checked unique-name admission.** `TableEnvelope::new` rejects duplicate names, but public fields and derived deserialization bypass it. Export relies on Arrow row-count/type/nullability checks, which do not enforce unique names. Equal-length raw duplicate-name tables therefore follow an accepting static path, unlike checked construction. The API specifies a validated input; choose the invariant owner and qualify raw duplicate-name and shape-error paths. Records: [003](arrow-interchange/03-envelope-boundary-and-batch-assembly.md).

- **Observed — Large UTF-8 columns can panic before mapped validation errors.** Both string variants use StringArray with i32 offsets. Pinned Arrow constructors use `expect` when aggregate present bytes exceed the offset range; the adapter has no preflight or chunking before this dependency panic. Even constructor-valid envelopes can reach it. Choose a checked size limit, larger canonical representation or explicit chunking policy and qualify its resource/error boundary. Distinguish the adapter's copying conversion from later shared-buffer host handoff in consumer guarantees. No multi-gigabyte reproduction was run. Records: [003](arrow-interchange/03-envelope-boundary-and-batch-assembly.md).

- **Observed — Metadata documentation describes a parser that is absent.** The crate README says malformed metadata JSON returns a validation error, but the API accepts typed `IndexMap<String, Value>` and serializes it without parsing embedded strings or validating a metadata schema. Correct that existing statement and separately specify any required semantic schema, version, provenance policy or validation. Select canonical serialization only if stable bytes are an explicit requirement; the record does not adopt it. Records: [002](arrow-interchange/02-schema-order-and-metadata.md).

- **Evidence — Payload and metadata transport guarantees need broader qualification.** The nine-variant fixture checks schema, two null counts, two roles and two JSON strings, but not all payload values, integer extrema, signed zero/nonfinite distinctions, all-present/all-null nullable cases or nested/omitted metadata. Python comparisons share ordinary finite producer values. Qualify the stated transport guarantees and host limits independently of financial model accuracy, including empty nullable columns, all declared roles, omitted/empty and nested metadata, and retention across supported consumers. Qualify contractual-Money precision at the producer boundary separately; Float64 plus currency metadata does not establish lossless monetary export. Records: [001](arrow-interchange/01-typed-values-and-null-semantics.md), [002](arrow-interchange/02-schema-order-and-metadata.md).

- **Evidence — Empty-shape and malformed-input tests cover only part of the batch contract.** The zero-column test uses a constructor-created 0×0 table. Explicit positive rows with zero columns, zero-row typed columns and raw length/count/name violations lack inspected adapter tests. Qualify these entry paths and mapped errors; the explicit row-count option alone is not runtime evidence. Records: [003](arrow-interchange/03-envelope-boundary-and-batch-assembly.md).
